\section*{Problem 113}
(Affine geometry).
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] (Chern). Establish Bernstein's theorem for affine geometry: any convex graph over affine space which is an affine maximal hypersurface must be a paraboloid.
\item[2.] Classify 3-dimensional compact affine-flat manifolds. In general, it is not known whether any compact affine-flat manifold has zero Euler number; see Milnor [M], Kostant-Sullivan [KS], Sullivan [S1], [S2], and Wood [W].
\end{itemize}

Status. Partially resolved, with three formulations that must be kept separate. Trudinger-Wang proved the smooth classical affine Bernstein theorem in graph dimension 2 [TW]. For smooth, locally uniformly convex classical affine-maximal hypersurfaces, the higher-dimensional problem remains open already in dimension 3. Their generalized nonsmooth notion has a counterexample in dimensions $n \geqslant 10$. Counterexamples for other values of the parameter in the broader affine-maximal-type equation do not by themselves disprove the classical affine-maximal conjecture [Du1, Du2].

The complete three-dimensional affine crystallographic groups were classified by Fried-Goldman [FG], but this is not a classification of all compact, possibly incomplete, affine 3-manifolds. Chern's Euler-characteristic conjecture for arbitrary compact affine manifolds also remains open; it is proved for complete affine manifolds [KS] and for special affine manifolds, whose holonomy preserves a volume form [Kl].

Milnor formulated the obstruction problem for flat affine structures in terms of flat torsion-free connections [M]. Kostant and Sullivan proved that the Euler characteristic of a complete affine space form is zero [KS], and Sullivan and Wood developed related characteristic class obstructions [S1], [S2], [W]. Trudinger and Wang later proved Chern's affine Bernstein conjecture for smooth surfaces. Their explicit high-dimensional example is affine maximal only in the generalized nonsmooth sense, and they explicitly left the existence of smooth high-dimensional counterexamples open [TW]. In the affine-manifold direction, Fried and Goldman classified three-dimensional affine crystallographic groups [FG], but the broader Chern conjecture that every compact affine manifold has zero Euler characteristic remains open.

Later developments split the two parts of the problem even further. For affine manifolds, Klingler proved Chern's conjecture for compact special affine manifolds, i.e. the case with parallel volume form [K1]. Bucher-Gelander proved Milnor-Wood type inequalities for manifolds locally modeled on products of hyperbolic planes, giving another important family where affine structures are obstructed [BG]. On the analytic Bernstein side, Du constructed non-quadratic, Euclidean-complete affinemaximal-type hypersurfaces for $N \geqslant 3$ and $\theta \in(1 / 2,(N-1) / N)$ [Du1], with further explicit examples in related ranges [Du2]. The classical affine-maximal exponent is $\theta=(N+1) /(N+2)$ and lies outside that interval. Sun-Xing-Xu study Calabi-affine and centroaffine Bernstein problems and construct complete examples in those related, but different, geometries [SXX].

\section*{Related open problems.}
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Prove or disprove the smooth classical affine Bernstein statement in every graph dimension $n \geqslant 3$.
\item[2.] Original direction. Decide Chern's Euler-characteristic conjecture for compact affine manifolds without completeness or parallel-volume hypotheses.
\item[3.] Reference-driven formulation. Classify compact affine-flat manifolds beyond the known low-dimensional and complete cases.
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine whether special affine, amenable-holonomy, locally symmetric, or producthyperbolic methods can be combined to prove new cases of Chern's affine Euler-characteristic conjecture [BG, Kl].
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine whether the nonsmooth $n \geqslant 10$ example of Trudinger-Wang can be regularized to a smooth, locally uniformly convex classical affine-maximal hypersurface, or prove a rigidity obstruction to such a regularization [TW].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Separate the classical affine-maximal exponent from affine-maximal-type, Calabi-affine and centroaffine variants, where higher-dimensional non-quadratic complete examples are known [Du1, Du2, SXX].
\item[7.] AI-generated formulation. Relate the analytic Bernstein problem for the affine maximal equation to holonomy and characteristic-class restrictions for compact affine manifolds.
\end{itemize}

\item[] [Du2] S.-Z. Du, Non-quadratic Euclidean complete affine maximal type hypersurfaces for $\theta \in(0,(N-1) / N]$, arXiv:2210.09127.

\section*{VII. General Relativity and the Yang-Mills Equation}

\section*{Problem 114}

This is the problem of "cosmic censorship", as coined by Penrose.
Let $M$ be a three-dimensional manifold equipped with a metric $g_{i j}$ and a symmetric tensor $h_{i j}$. Assume that $g_{i j}$ and $h_{i j}$ satisfy the compatibility requirement necessary for them to represent the induced metric and second fundamental form, respectively, that $M$ would inherit as a spacelike hypersurface of four-dimensional asymptotically flat spacetime satisfying the (vacuum) Einstein field equations.

In the study of global solutions to the vacuum field equation with Cauchy data $g_{i j}$ and $h_{i j}$ on $M$, one wishes to know the nature of the singularities of the solution obtained. Perhaps the most important open problem in general relativity is this:

Is it true that generically a singularity will have a horizon? (Is there no "naked" singularity?)
This question amounts to asking if the future can be theoretically predicted.
One should consult the book of Hawking and Ellis [HE] for background on this problem.

Status. Open in the full generic form. Cosmic censorship is not a single theorem but a family of weak and strong formulations, and each formulation requires precise hypotheses on asymptotics, matter model, regularity and genericity. Without such hypotheses simple counterexamples occur [Ch].

Hawking and Ellis gave the classical causal and singularity-theorem background [HE]. Christodoulou and Klainerman proved the nonlinear stability of Minkowski space for small asymptotically flat vacuum data [CK], and Lindblad and Rodnianski later gave a wave-coordinate proof of the same stability theorem [LR]. Andersson and Moncrief proved future stability for certain expanding Einstein flows [AM]. Ringström established strong cosmic censorship for $\mathbb{T}^{3}$-Gowdy spacetimes $[\mathrm{R}]$. These theorems are major confirmations in special regimes, but they do not settle Penrose's generic censorship problem. The nonlinear stability of the Kerr family for small angular momentum has also been proved through the Klainerman-Szeftel and Giorgi-Klainerman-Szeftel program [KS, GKS]. This is a major black-hole stability theorem, but it is not by itself a theorem that generic large-data gravitational collapse forms a horizon; final-state stability and weak cosmic censorship are logically distinct statements. Current literature still treats strong and weak cosmic censorship as central open problems, with different behavior in asymptotically flat, de Sitter, charged, rotating, symmetric, and matter-coupled settings.

The Kerr and de Sitter stability literatures sharpen the modern landscape. Hintz-Vasy proved nonlinear stability of the Kerr-de Sitter family for slowly rotating black holes [HV], and Häfner-Hintz-Vasy proved linear stability of slowly rotating

Kerr black holes in the asymptotically flat setting [HHV]. The Giorgi-Klainerman-Szeftel proof of nonlinear stability for slowly rotating Kerr in vacuum is therefore part of a broader program linking final-state stability and black-hole exterior dynamics [KS, GKS]. Recent work reaches the full subextremal Kerr-de Sitter range only conditionally on mode stability [HPV].

Strong cosmic censorship is sensitive to the regularity class used for extension. Dafermos-Luk prove $C^{0}$ stability of a portion of the Kerr Cauchy horizon, while Luk-Oh prove a generic $C^{2}$-inextendibility statement for the spherically symmetric Einstein-Maxwell-scalar model [DL, LO]. Thus a formulation may fail at $C^{0}$ and hold at a higher regularity threshold. On the weak side, An-Tan prove a generic spherical-symmetry result for the Einstein-Maxwell-charged-scalar system [AT]. None of these symmetry-reduced or perturbative theorems settles the full asymptotically flat vacuum conjecture.

\section*{Related open problems.}
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Formulate a generic weak cosmic censorship theorem for asymptotically flat vacuum initial data and prove that singularities visible from null infinity are nongeneric.
\item[2.] Original direction. Prove strong cosmic censorship with the correct regularity threshold for physically natural Einstein-matter models.
\item[3.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine which matter perturbations preserve censorship and which produce stable naked singularities.
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Connect nonlinear stability of slowly rotating Kerr and Kerr-de Sitter spacetimes with precise final-state and weak-censorship formulations [GKS, HHV, HV].
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Identify the regularity thresholds at which Cauchy horizons violate or preserve strong cosmic censorship in charged, rotating, and cosmological models [DL, LO].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Remove spherical symmetry from the charged-scalar weak-censorship theorem, or identify the first new obstruction created by angular momentum [AT].
\item[7.] AI-generated formulation. Compare censorship thresholds under asymptotically flat, asymptotically de Sitter and spatially compact boundary conditions in one common function-space framework.
\end{itemize}

\section*{Problem 115}

The splitting theorem of Cheeger and Gromoll [CG] says that if a Riemannian manifold $M$ of nonnegative Ricci curvature contains a line $\gamma$ (i.e., an absolutely minimizing geodesic), then $M$ decomposes isometrically as a Riemannian product $\mathbb{R} \times N$, the first factor being represented by $\gamma$.

It would be of interest in studying the structure of space-time to prove that a geodesically complete Lorentzian 4-manifold of nonnegative Ricci curvature in the timelike direction which contains an absolutely maximizing timelike geodesic is isometrically the cross product of that geodesic and a spacelike hypersurface.

Status. Solved under the standard global hypotheses of the Lorentzian splitting theorem. Newman's theorem proves Yau's original timelike-geodesically-complete formulation [N]. Galloway proved the complementary globally hyperbolic formulation without assuming timelike geodesic completeness [G].

Cheeger and Gromoll's Riemannian splitting theorem is the model [CG]. In the Lorentzian setting the Ricci nonnegativity condition is replaced by the timelike convergence condition, and a line is replaced by a complete timelike line. Under the strong energy condition, the existence of such a line and either timelike geodesic completeness or global hyperbolicity gives an isometric splitting $\mathbb{R} \times S$ with $S$ complete [N, G, BGMOS]. Later work, including Galloway-Vega's achronal-limit approach, clarified variants with weaker completeness or curvature hypotheses [GV]. Recent work moves the problem in two directions. Woolgar-Wylie proved Lorentzian splitting and singularity theorems under Bakry-Emery curvature-dimension hypotheses, where the equality case can become a warped product rather than the rigid product in the classical theorem [WW]. Beran-Ohanyan-Rott-Solis proved a synthetic splitting theorem for globally hyperbolic Lorentzian length spaces with nonnegative timelike curvature and a complete timelike line [BORS]. Braun-Calisti and Braun-McCann developed timelike Ricci lower bounds through optimal transport and timelike curvature-dimension conditions in low-regularity or nonsmooth spacetimes [BC], [BM]. A unified elliptic proof now covers the smooth Galloway and Newman alternatives [BGMOS], Galloway has replaced pointwise timelike Ricci nonnegativity by an averaged condition along timelike rays [G25], and a weighted globally hyperbolic splitting theorem is available for $C^{1}$ metrics [BGMOS2]. Thus the original smooth problem is settled, but its natural descendants concern how much regularity, completeness and pointwise Ricci control can be removed while preserving a genuine product conclusion.

\section*{Related open problems.}
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Determine whether Galloway's averaged Ricci condition along every timelike ray is sharp, and whether it can be replaced by a condition imposed only along the given line [G25].
\item[2.] Original direction. Extend the $C^{1}$ globally hyperbolic theorem to the Newman alternative based solely on timelike geodesic completeness [BGMOS2].
\item[3.] Original direction. Understand the borderline cases where timelike lines exist only after taking limits of maximizing segments.
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine when Bakry-Emery or curvature-dimension timelike convergence hypotheses give an isometric product, and when the best possible rigidity is only a warped product [WW].
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Push below $C^{1}$ to locally Lipschitz or distributional Lorentzian metrics using synthetic timelike Ricci lower bounds, and compare the conclusion with the $C^{1}$ theorem [BC, BM, BGMOS2].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Identify the exact relationship between Lorentzian length-space splitting and smooth Lorentzian splitting by approximation, regularization and stability of timelike lines [BORS].
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}
\item[7.] AI-generated formulation. Classify almost-splitting phenomena: if the timelike Ricci curvature is nearly nonnegative and a very long almost-maximizing timelike line exists, quantify the closeness to a Lorentzian product on large causal diamonds.
\item[8.] AI-generated formulation. Develop null and timelike versions of splitting in one framework, especially for spacetimes where complete timelike lines degenerate to null lines at infinity.
\end{itemize}

Problem 116
Prove that a static stellar model is isometric to a sphere. See Lindblom [Lin] for the case when the model has uniform density. S. Hawking demonstrated that a static black hole is axially symmetric, but his argument is based in part on physical reasoning. From the work of Israel, Hawking, Carter, and Robinson, one knows that a stationary, rotating black hole must be the Kerr black hole (see [Ro]). Can one make a similar statement about a charged stationary black hole? If the metric is Riemannian, there are similar questions. Lapedes (these proceedings) points out that Robinson's method does not apply, but Israel's approach still works, in the static case.

Status. Partially resolved. The stellar and black-hole parts must be separated. Static asymptotically flat perfect-fluid stars are spherical for broad physically important classes of equations of state, but the unrestricted fluid-ball conjecture still requires equation-of-state and boundary hypotheses. Stationary black-hole uniqueness likewise depends on dimension, matter model, horizon structure, regularity and asymptotic assumptions.

Robinson proved uniqueness of the Kerr black hole in the classical stationary vacuum setting [Ro]. Lindblom proved spherical symmetry for static uniform-density stars [Lin]. Beig-Simon and Masood-ul-Alam proved spherical symmetry theorems for static perfect-fluid stars under suitable conditions [BS], [M]. These results should not be read as a theorem for an arbitrary perfect fluid. Coutinho-Leandro-Reis give a Robinson-divergence proof under an explicit equation of state and state the unrestricted fluid-ball conjecture separately [CLR]. Chruściel and Tod treated static electro-vacuum black holes [CT], while Hollands-Ishibashi-Wald generalized Hawking's axisymmetry theorem to higher dimensions [HIW]. Chruściel-Costa-Heusler survey the modern uniqueness picture and emphasize that the classical "no-hair" form must be refined in broader theories [CCH]. Bunting-Masood-ul-Alam's conformal argument gives a central proof of static vacuum black-hole uniqueness [BMA]. For charged stationary black holes, the modern electrovacuum classification is close to the Kerr-Newman answer only
after strong hypotheses are imposed: analyticity, $I^{+}$-regularity, connectedness or a precise treatment of degenerate horizons. Lopes Costa obtained a classification of stationary analytic electrovacuum black holes in terms of Weinstein solutions and recovered Kerr-Newman uniqueness for connected horizons [LC]; Amsel-Horowitz-Marolf-Roberts and Chruściel-Nguyen treated important extremal or degenerate Kerr-Newman uniqueness cases [AHMR], [CN]. Thus the original charged question has substantial affirmative answers, but the sharp geometric hypotheses remain part of the modern problem. Chruściel's 2023 note completes the connected, analytic, suitably regular four-dimensional stationary vacuum statement and clarifies which near-horizon ingredients do not need analyticity; it does not remove analyticity from the global stationary theorem [C23].

\section*{Related open problems.}
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Determine the sharp hypotheses under which static stellar models must be spherically symmetric.
\item[2.] Original direction. Classify stationary charged black holes under minimal regularity, connectedness and nondegeneracy assumptions.
\item[3.] Original direction. Extend black-hole uniqueness theorems to higher-dimensional and nonlinear matter models, or exhibit the precise failures.
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Identify which uniqueness results survive if asymptotic flatness is replaced by weaker geometric falloff conditions.
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Remove analyticity or connected-horizon assumptions from stationary electrovacuum Kerr-Newman uniqueness, or construct explicit counterexamples to the naive formulation [LC], [CN].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Extend the conformal positive-mass method of static black-hole uniqueness to static perfect-fluid stars with the weakest physically natural equation of state hypotheses [BMA], [BS], [M].
\item[7.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine the precise relation between axisymmetry theorems and uniqueness theorems in higher-dimensional Einstein-Maxwell or Einstein-matter systems [HIW].
\item[8.] AI-generated formulation. Build a "hypothesis map" for black-hole uniqueness: horizon connectedness, nondegeneracy, analyticity, topology, matter model, asymptotic geometry and dimension should each be marked as removable, essential or unknown.
\item[9.] AI-generated formulation. Formulate a quantitative near-uniqueness theorem: if a static star nearly saturates a spherical-symmetry criterion, prove closeness to a spherically symmetric Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff model.
\item[10.] Reference-driven formulation. Replace the differential equation-of-state inequalities in known fluid-ball theorems by the weakest causality, monotonicity and free-boundary assumptions [CLR].
\end{itemize}

\section*{Problem 117}

Prove that any Yang-Mills field on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ is either self-dual or anti-self-dual.
See the paper of Bourguignon and Lawson [BL] in this volume. Atiyah, Drinfeld, Hitchin, and Manin [A+] have classified the self- and anti-self-dual solutions.

Status. False. There exist Yang-Mills connections on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ which are neither self-dual nor anti-self-dual.
Atiyah-Drinfeld-Hitchin-Manin classified the instanton solutions, namely the self-dual and anti-self-dual Yang-Mills fields [A+]. This does not classify all Yang-Mills fields. Sibner-Sibner- Uhlenbeck proved the existence of non-self-dual Yang-Mills solutions [SSU]. Sadun and Segert later produced explicit non-self-dual Yang-Mills connections with nonzero Chern number [SS]. Thus the original assertion fails, while the instanton sector remains the distinguished absolute-minimizer sector in four-dimensional gauge theory. Subsequent analytic work has shifted the emphasis from the false dichotomy "instantons or anti-instantons" to the structure of the full Yang-Mills critical set. Feehan proved discreteness results for the possible Yang-Mills energies over closed four-manifolds and energy-gap theorems near the ground-state strata [F]. This supports a more Morsetheoretic version of the problem: understand the non-instanton critical points, their indices, their bubbling compactifications and their relation to the instanton moduli spaces. In this form, the problem is still useful even though the original claim is false. Dayaprema-Waldron prove a parabolic gap statement: on an $S U(r)$-bundle of charge $\kappa$ over $\mathbb{S}^{4}$, the space of connections with Yang-Mills energy below $4 \pi^{2}(|\kappa|+2)$ deformation-retracts under Yang-Mills flow to the instantons [DW]. Hence the first non-instanton critical level is constrained from below, but the critical set above that threshold is not classified.

\section*{Related open problems.}
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Classify non-self-dual Yang-Mills critical points on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ up to gauge and conformal symmetry.
\item[2.] Original direction. Understand the Morse-theoretic role of non-self-dual critical points for the Yang-Mills functional.
\item[3.] Original direction. Determine which compact four-manifolds admit explicit non-instanton Yang-Mills connections with prescribed topological charge.
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Compute the Morse index and nullity of the Sibner-Sibner-Uhlenbeck and Sadun-Segert non-self-dual examples, and locate them inside the full Yang-Mills energy spectrum [SSU], [SS], [F].
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine whether the Yang-Mills energy spectrum on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ for a fixed compact structure group and topological class can be described explicitly, beyond the general discreteness theorem [F].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Describe bubble-tree limits of sequences of non-self-dual Yang-Mills critical points on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ and compare them with Uhlenbeck compactification of instanton moduli spaces.
\item[7.] AI-generated formulation. Build an "atlas" of non-instanton critical points on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ : symmetry type, structure group, Chern number, energy level, Morse index and degeneration behavior.
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}
\item[8.] AI-generated formulation. Search for min-max constructions of non-self-dual Yang-Mills connections whose critical values lie between consecutive instanton energy levels.
\item[9.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine whether the parabolic gap threshold is attained by a non-self-dual critical point in each rank and charge, and classify the equality and bubbling scenarios [DW].
\end{itemize}

Problem 118
Prove that the moduli space of the self-dual fields on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ with a fixed Pontrjagin number is connected. Prove that the Pontrjagin number of a $L^{2}$-integrable gauge field on $\mathbb{R}^{4}$ is an integer.

Both Problems 117 and 118 are very well known. See the excellent article of Atiyah [A].

Status. Solved in the standard $S U(r)$ instanton setting. The two assertions require different analytic theorems. Taubes proved path-connectedness of the instanton moduli spaces on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$ [T]. Uhlenbeck's 1985 theory of Chern classes of Sobolev connections proves integrality for an arbitrary connection on $\mathbb{R}^{4}$ whose curvature is in $L^{2}$ [UC]. Her 1982 removable singularity theorem [U] gives the stronger smooth compactification statement when the connection also satisfies the Yang-Mills equation.

Atiyah's ICM article records the gauge-theoretic formulation and the role of instantons [A]. Uhlenbeck proved that a finiteenergy Yang-Mills field on a punctured four-ball extends smoothly after gauge [U]. For a general finite-energy connection no Euler-Lagrange equation is available; the integer is instead obtained by constructing the associated Sobolev bundle and identifying its Chern-Weil number with an integral Chern class [UC]. Taubes proved path-connectedness for the relevant instanton moduli spaces [T]. Later developments put these solved statements into a larger moduli-space framework. Donaldson-Kronheimer give the standard gauge-theoretic treatment of instanton compactification and four-manifold applications [DK]. Nakajima's quiver-variety theory extends the ADHM viewpoint and relates instanton moduli spaces to representation theory and singular hyperkähler geometry [N]. Recent mathematical-physics work computes Hilbert series and Coulomb-branch descriptions of instanton moduli spaces, including higher charge and nonclassical groups [HMR], [CFHM]. These results do not change the connectedness and integrality answers, but they give more explicit algebraic invariants of the same moduli spaces. Dayaprema-Waldron give a new differential-geometric proof valid for every $S U(r)$-bundle: a sharp Yang-Mills sublevel deformation-retracts to the instanton space, and Taubes' charge-subtraction/gluing induction then gives path-connectedness [DW].

Related open problems.
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Describe the singular strata and compactifications of Yang-Mills moduli spaces in a way adapted to explicit geometry.
\item[2.] Original direction. Compare the connectedness and compactification picture for instantons with the corresponding Seiberg-Witten moduli spaces.
\item[3.] Original direction. Extend removable-singularity and integrality results to higher-dimensional Yang-Mills fields under sharp analytic hypotheses.
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Give an explicit stratified description of the Uhlenbeck compactification of instanton moduli spaces on $\mathbb{S}^{4}$, compatible with the ADHM and quiver-variety models [DK], [N].
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Compute Hilbert series, intersection cohomology or other algebraic invariants of fixedcharge instanton moduli spaces and relate them to geometric compactification strata [HMR], [CFHM].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Identify the sharp finite-energy hypotheses under which removable singularity and integrality remain true for Yang-Mills fields in dimensions greater than four.
\item[7.] AI-generated formulation. Produce a table, by compact structure group and instanton number, comparing connectedness, smoothness, singular strata, known algebraic models and known cohomological invariants.
\item[8.] AI-generated formulation. Look for a direct path construction between two ADHM data sets of the same charge which keeps the path inside a controlled regular stratum except at explicitly described wall crossings.
\item[9.] Reference-driven formulation. Compare the 2026 Yang-Mills-flow deformation retraction with the algebraic Uhlenbeck compactification and determine whether it controls intersection cohomology of the boundary strata [DW], [CFHM].
\end{itemize}

Problem 119
Physicists have a notion of asymptotically flat manifolds (see [SY], for example). The definition depends heavily on the choice of coordinate system and is not intrinsic. If one replaces the definition by requiring the curvature to decay suitably, do we obtain an equivalent condition?

Status. Partially resolved, but curvature decay alone is not equivalent to asymptotic flatness. Under standard decay and volume-growth hypotheses, Bartnik gave an intrinsic treatment of mass and asymptotic coordinates [B], Chruściel analyzed the corresponding Hamiltonian boundary conditions [C], and Bando-Kasue-Nakajima constructed ALE coordinates at infinity from fast curvature decay and maximal volume growth [BKN]. Asymptotic flatness is the trivial-group case; topology at infinity and further regularity cannot be omitted.

Schoen-Yau's positive mass theorem uses the classical coordinate definition of asymptotic flatness [SY]. Bartnik showed that the mass has a geometric meaning under appropriate falloff and coordinate change hypotheses [B]. Chruściel gave an alternative Hamiltonian analysis of boundary conditions at spatial infinity [C]. Bando-Kasue-Nakajima supplied a Riemannian coordinate construction at infinity from fast curvature decay and volume growth [BKN]. Thus the desired implication is true in important precise regimes only after one controls the finite group at infinity and the analytic hypotheses. Curvature decay by itself also contains no information about the extrinsic curvature of an Einstein initial data set or the parity needed for some asymptotic
charges. More recent work has shifted part of the question from "which coordinates may be chosen?" to "which coordinates and asymptotic charges are geometrically selected?" Nerz proved geometric characterizations of asymptotic flatness and linear momentum using CMC-type covers near infinity [N]. Cederbaum-Metzger review and announce results on geometrically defined asymptotic coordinates, asymptotic foliations and the geometric meaning of mass, energy, linear momentum, angular momentum and center of mass [CM]. Thus the coordinate-dependence problem remains active even after BKN-type coordinate existence: one wants canonical, invariantly selected asymptotic charts and charges under weak falloff hypotheses.

\section*{Related open problems.}
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Determine the weakest curvature-decay and volume-growth hypotheses that force a useful asymptotically flat coordinate system.
\item[2.] Original direction. Extend intrinsic asymptotic-flatness criteria to lower regularity initial data used in the Einstein constraint equations.
\item[3.] Original direction. Compare spatial-infinity and null-infinity definitions of asymptotic flatness in a single geometric framework.
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Determine whether CMC/STCMC or related asymptotic foliations characterize asymptotic flatness under weaker hypotheses than current geometric characterization theorems [N], [CM].
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Compare BKN harmonic coordinates, Bartnik harmonic coordinates and geometrically selected asymptotic coordinates in the same decay scale [B], [BKN], [CM].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Develop intrinsic criteria that recover not only asymptotic flatness but also ADM linear momentum, angular momentum and center of mass without imposing a preferred coordinate system at the outset [N], [CM].
\item[7.] AI-generated formulation. Formulate a stability theorem: if curvature decay and volume growth are almost compatible with an asymptotically flat end, quantify how close the end is to admitting a canonical asymptotic chart.
\item[8.] AI-generated formulation. Build a comparison table of coordinate-free definitions at spatial infinity and at null infinity, recording which conserved quantities each definition controls.
\item[9.] Reference-driven formulation. Find intrinsic conditions that force the finite ALE group at infinity to be trivial, thereby upgrading BKN coordinates to genuinely asymptotically flat coordinates [BKN].
\end{itemize}

\section*{Problem 120}

Given an asymptotically flat space, can one give a good definition of total angular momentum? What would the relationship be with total mass? (See [Pe].)

Status. Partially resolved, with several definitions depending on the asymptotic setting. Classical ADM-type angular momentum was analyzed by Beig-Ó Murchadha [Bo], and Chen-Wang-Yau gave a quasi-local definition whose spatial-infinity limit supplies total angular momentum under weak asymptotic hypotheses [CWY]. At null infinity, Chen-Wang-Wang-Yau developed a supertranslation-invariant formulation [CWWY].

Penrose listed total angular momentum as one of the fundamental problems of classical general relativity [Pe]. Beig-Ó Murchadha identified the Poincaré symmetry and Hamiltonian charges at spatial infinity [Bo]. Szabados determined the corresponding falloff and parity conditions and the Poincaré transformation law [Sz]. Chruściel-Delay used angular momentum in the constraint gluing framework [CD]. Chen-Wang-Yau derived quasi-local conserved quantities from optimal isometric embeddings and proved that their limits give total angular momentum, center of mass and the expected dynamical formulas for isolated systems [CWY]. Later work at null infinity removes the supertranslation ambiguity in the angular momentum and center-of-mass definitions [CWWY]. Chen-Wang-Wang-Yau subsequently verified the supertranslation invariance of their angular momentum at null infinity in double-null gauge [CWWY2]. Javadinezhad-Porrati proposed supertranslation- invariant angular-momentum flux formulae for gravitational scattering and analyzed remaining covariance/frame-fixing puzzles [JP1], [JP2]. Cederbaum-Metzger's geometric-coordinate program also links angular momentum and center of mass to asymptotic foliations at spatial infinity [CM]. Javadinezhad-Porrati have since given a covariant prescription for fixing both supertranslation and translation frames and clarified the difference between invariant Lorentz charges and generators of BMS coordinate transformations [JP3]. The modern frontier is therefore not merely the existence of a definition, but compatibility among spatial infinity, null infinity, flux laws, BMS covariance, numerical relativity and black-hole final-state questions. On the inequality side, Han-Khuri-Weinstein-Xiong prove a multiple-black-hole mass-angular-momentum inequality modulo an explicit extreme nohair alternative, still assuming axisymmetry and maximal initial data [HKWX]. A 2025 preprint of Xu claims an axisymmetric angular-momentum Penrose inequality [Xu]. Neither result removes symmetry, so no general mass-angular-momentum relation follows from these theorems.

\section*{Related open problems.}
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Original direction. Compare all major notions of angular momentum for general asymptotically flat initial data without parity assumptions.
\item[2.] Original direction. Prove sharp flux and balance laws for angular momentum through null infinity in low regularity radiating spacetimes.
\item[3.] Original direction. Relate total angular momentum, total mass and center of mass to black-hole uniqueness and stability problems.
\item[4.] Reference-driven formulation. Develop a computable version of the Chen-Wang-Yau angular momentum for numerical relativity data sets with controlled error estimates.
\item[5.] Reference-driven formulation. Compare spatial-infinity CWY angular momentum, null-infinity CWY angular momentum and geometrically selected asymptotic-coordinate definitions under one set of decay and radiation hypotheses [CWY], [CWWY], [CWWY2], [CM].
\item[6.] Reference-driven formulation. Decide which supertranslation-invariant flux formula has the correct covariance, framefixing and scattering behavior in nonlinear radiating spacetimes [JP1], [JP2].
\item[7.] Reference-driven formulation. Establish mass-angular momentum inequalities and balance laws without axial symmetry, or identify the minimal replacement for symmetry.
\item[8.] AI-generated formulation. Create benchmark families of asymptotically flat numerical initial data for which ADM, CWY, Bondi-Sachs and scattering-based angular momenta can all be computed and compared.
\item[9.] AI-generated formulation. Formulate a final-state angular-momentum theorem: prove that the total angular momentum radiated through null infinity plus the final Kerr angular momentum equals the initial angular momentum in a fully geometric definition.
\end{itemize}
10. Reference-driven formulation. Remove axisymmetry from mass-angular-momentum inequalities by finding a geometric replacement for the twist-potential harmonic-map reduction [HKWX], [Xu].
